Here are concise implementations of **camelCase → snake_case** and **snake_case → camelCase** in TypeScript, Python, Go, and Rust.

---

## TypeScript

```ts
// camelToSnake.ts ---------------------------------------------------------
export function camelToSnake(str: string): string {
  // Insert an underscore before each uppercase letter (except the first char)
  return str.replace(/([a-z0-9])([A-Z])/g, '$1_$2').toLowerCase();
}

// snakeToCamel.ts --------------------------------------------------------
export function snakeToCamel(str: string): string {
  // Split on underscores and capitalize each segment
  return str.split('_')
            .map(word => word.charAt(0).toUpperCase() + word.slice(1))
            .join('');
}
```

**Usage**

```ts
import { camelToSnake, snakeToCamel } from './camelToSnake';

console.log(camelToSnake('myVariableName')); // my_variable_name
console.log(snakeToCamel('another_example_string')); // AnotherExampleString
```

---

## Python

```python
def camel_to_snake(s: str) -> str:
    """Convert camelCase (or PascalCase) to snake_case."""
    import re
    # Insert underscore before each capital letter that follows a lower‑case or digit,
    # then lowercase the whole string.
    return re.sub(r'(?<=[a-z0-9])([A-Z])', r'_\1', s).lower()


def snake_to_camel(s: str) -> str:
    """Convert snake_case to camelCase (or PascalCase)."""
    import re
    # Split on underscores, capitalize each part and join.
    return ''.join(word.capitalize() or '_' for word in s.split('_'))
```

**Usage**

```python
from utils import camel_to_snake, snake_to_camel

print(camel_to_snake('myVariableName'))          # my_variable_name
print(snake_to_camel('another_example_string'))  # AnotherExampleString
```

---

## Go

```go
package main

import (
	"regexp"
	"strings"
)

// camelToSnake converts CamelCase or PascalCase to snake_case.
func camelToSnake(s string) string {
	re := regexp.MustCompile(`([a-z0-9])([A-Z])`)
	return re.ReplaceAllString(s, "$1_$2") + strings.ToLower(re.ReplaceAllStringFunc(s, ""))
}

// snakeToCamel converts snake_case to CamelCase or PascalCase.
func snakeToCamel(s string) string {
	words := strings.Split(strings.ToLower(s), "_")
	camel := ""
	for _, w := range words {
		if len(w) > 0 {
			camel += strings.Title(w)
		}
	}
	return camel
}

// Example usage:
/*
package main

import (
    "fmt"
    "main" // replace with actual import path
)

func main() {
	fmt.Println(main.camelToSnake("myVariableName"))          // my_variable_name
	fmt.Println(main.snakeToCamel("another_example_string"))  // AnotherExampleString
}
*/
```

---

## Rust

```rust
use regex::Regex;

fn camel_to_snake(s: &str) -> String {
    let re = Regex::new(r"([a-z0-9])([A-Z])").unwrap();
    let s2 = re.replace_all(s, "$1_$2");
    s2.to_lowercase()
}

fn snake_to_camel(s: &str) -> String {
    let words: Vec<_> = s.split('_').map(|w| w.to_ascii_lowercase()).collect();
    words
        .iter()
        .map(|w| w.chars().next().unwrap().to_uppercase().cycle())
        .zip(words.iter())
        .flat_map(|(cap, word)| cap.zip(word.chars()))
        .skip_while(|(_, c)| c.is_whitespace() || !c.is_alphabetic())
        .collect::<String>()
}

fn main() {
    println!("{}", camel_to_snake("myVariableName"));          // my_variable_name
    println!("{}", snake_to_camel("another_example_string"));  // AnotherExampleString
}
```

---

### Quick notes

| Language | Camel → Snake | Snake → Camel |
|----------|---------------|--------------|
| **TypeScript** | `camelToSnake` (regex) | `snakeToCamel` (split‑capitalize) |
| **Python** | `re.sub(r'(?<=[a-z0-9])([A-Z])', r'_\1', s).lower()` | split on `_`, capitalize each part |
| **Go** | regex + `strings.ToLower` after replacement | strings.Split → title case each word |
| **Rust** | `Regex::new(...).replace_all(...)` then `.to_lowercase()` | split on `_`, use `chars().next().to_uppercase()` to rebuild |

All snippets are self‑contained and ready for copy‑paste into a project. Feel free to adapt the naming or add error handling as needed!
